# Proposed OEIS comments: A252125 to A253403

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A252125

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A252125>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [6964](../papers/06501-07000/06964-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) +140*a(n-3) -140*a(n-4) -6911*a(n-6) +6911*a(n-7) +152837*a(n-9) -152837*a(n-10) -1442504*a(n-12) +1442504*a(n-13) +2343083*a(n-15) -2343083*a(n-16) +39334064*a(n-18) -39334064*a(n-19) -202724388*a(n-21) +202724388*a(n-22) +108881376*a(n-24) -108881376*a(n-25) +1256967648*a(n-27) -1256967648*a(n-28) -3002648832*a(n-30) +3002648832*a(n-31) +2388828672*a(n-33) -2388828672*a(n-34) -597196800*a(n-36) +597196800*a(n-37) for n>42

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 048 576 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 42. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A252132

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A252132>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [9785](../papers/09501-10000/09785-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + a(n-2) + a(n-4) - 5*a(n-5) - 7*a(n-6) - 3*a(n-7) + 4*a(n-8) + 6*a(n-9) + 2*a(n-10) for n>12.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(172 - 210*x - 231*x^2 - 113*x^3 - 350*x^4 + 333*x^5 + 933*x^6 + 792*x^7 - 86*x^8 - 650*x^9 - 324*x^10 - 32*x^11) / ((1 - x)*(1 + x)*(1 - x - x^2)*(1 - x - 3*x^3 - 4*x^4 - 4*x^5 - 2*x^6)). - _Colin Barker_, Dec 01 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 4 096 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 12. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A252243>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [4252](../papers/04001-04500/04252-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical column recurrences are true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A252544>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [4250](../papers/04001-04500/04250-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: T(n,k) is symmetric for n and k both greater than 1.
> Empirical for column k:
> Empirical for row n:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical column recurrences are true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. ## A252870

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A252870>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [5509](../papers/05501-06000/05509-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (1/24)*n^4 + (5/12)*n^3 - (13/24)*n^2 - (11/12)*n + 1.
> Empirical: G.f.: -x^2*(1+3*x-4*x^2+x^3) / (x-1)^5. - _R. J. Mathar_, Nov 21 2015

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 15 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A252970

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A252970>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [5506](../papers/05501-06000/05506-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (1/720)*n^6 + (7/240)*n^5 - (1/144)*n^4 - (31/48)*n^3 + (271/180)*n^2 - (53/60)*n = n*(n-1)*(n-2)*(n^3+24*n^2+65*n-318)/720.
> Empirical: G.f.: -x^3*(1+6*x-9*x^2+3*x^3) / (x-1)^7 . - _R. J. Mathar_, Nov 23 2015

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 21 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A253018

Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [5209](../papers/05001-05500/05209-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-3) for n>5.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(25 + 37*x + 51*x^2 - 12*x^3 - 6*x^4) / (1 - 3*x^3). - _Colin Barker_, Dec 08 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. ```

## A253152

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A253152>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [12182](../papers/12001-12500/12182-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) -2*a(n-2) for n>5.
> Empirical: a(n) = 9*2^(n-1) + 37 for n>3.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(16 - 9*x - 16*x^2 - 20*x^3 - 8*x^4) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 2*x)). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 20 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 16 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 476 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 16. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
